## 1. Introduction et Principes d'Herboristerie Néotropicale

La forêt tropicale amazonienne abrite la plus haute biodiversité phytochimique de la planète. Les végétaux du bassin amazonien ont développé un arsenal de métabolites secondaires de haute puissance :
- **Lignanes et Polyphénols Spécifiques** : Antiviraux hépatotropes, néphroprotecteurs et antioxydants.
- **Alcaloïdes Oxindoliques et Isoquinoléiques** : Modulateurs immunitaires, antispasmodiques musculaires et relaxants vasculaires.
- **Naphtoquinones et Diterpènes** : Fongicides systémiques, bactéricides et régulateurs du biofilm intestinal.
- **Tanins Condensés et Proanthocyanidines** : Astringents des muqueuses, protecteurs du glycocalyx et cicatrisants.

Dans l'approche vitaliste de VitalTrack, ces plantes sauvages sont employées comme des **catalyseurs émonctoriels** stimulant la filtration rénale, le drainage lymphatique, la vidange biliaire et le désencombrement des muqueuses.

### 1. Abuta (*Cissampelos pareira*)
- **Famille Botanique** : Menispermaceae
- **Noms Usuels / Synonymes** : abuta, abutua, barbasco, imchich masha, butua, false pareira, pareira, aristoloche lobee, bejuco de raton, feuille coeur, liane patte cheval, gasing-gasing
- **Parties Utilisées** : Feuilles, écorce, racines ou plante entière
- **Catégorie Thérapeutique & Tropisme** : Reins & Émonctoires (Drainage Rénal & Lithiases)
- **Source Primaire Rain-Tree** : [Abuta Monograph on Rain-Tree.com](https://www.rain-tree.com/abuta.htm)
- **Principes Phytochimiques Actifs** :
  - Cissampelos plants
  - including abuta
  - contain a group of plant chemicals called isoquinoline alkaloids
  - Since the late 1960s
  - these chemicals have received a great deal of attention and research
  - Out of thirty-eight alkaloids thus far discovered in abuta
  - called tetrandrine
  - is the most well documented
- **Indications & Cibles Thérapeutiques** :
  - therefore
  - An alkaloid in abuta
  - tetrandrine
  - In studies with mice
  - Those seeking to get pregnant should avoid the use of abuta
- **Mécanismes & Pharmacodynamie (Leslie Taylor)** :
1. **Actions Principales (Rain-Tree)** : 1. Abuta has been documented to lower blood pressure in two animal studies; therefore, abuta is probably contraindicated for people with low blood pressure. An alkaloid in abuta, tetrandrine, has been documented to have various actions on heart function in animals and humans. Those with a heart condition or taking heart medications should consult with their doctor before using this plant. 2. Abuta has demonstrated to be a uterine relaxant and traditionally employed as a childbirth aid. A pregnant woman should use it only under the supervision of a qualified healthcare practitioner. 3. In studies with mice, abuta extracts have demonstrated female anti-fertility activity by modulating the hormones specifically responsible for fertility. Those seeking to get pregnant should avoid the use of abuta.
2. **Recherche Clinique & Activité Biologique** : There has been a significant amount of research published on abuta and/or its main active chemicals in the last ten years. So far, research has confirmed, in vivo or in vitro , that abuta has pain-relieving, fever-reducing, anti-inflammatory, antiviral, antibacterial, anti-allergic, bronchodilator, immunomodulatory, memory-enhancing, antidepressant, neuroprotective, anti-obesity, antimalarial, ant...
3. **Usage Ethnobotanique Traditionnel** : Abuta ( Cissampelos pariera ) is commonly referred to as the midwives' herb throughout South America because of its long history of use for all types of women's ailments. The vine or root of abuta is used in tropical countries to prevent a threatened miscarriage and to stop uterine hemorrhages after childbirth. Midwives in the Amazon still carry ab...
- **Posologie & Modes d'Administration** :
  - **Dosage Standard** : stops bleeding kills bacteria Vine Wood balances menstruation enhances memory Decoction: 1 cup 2-3 times daily relieves pain prevents convulsions Tincture: 2-3 ml 2-3 times daily reduces cramps fights free radicals Capsules: 1-2 g 2-3 times daily relaxes muscles prevents ulcers stops inflammation reduces mucous increases urination reduces fever protects cells protects liver lowers blood pressure balances hormones inhibits tumors kills viruses
  - **Infusion / Décoction** : 1 cuillère à café à soupe de plante séchée par tasse d'eau chaude, infuser 10-15 min.
  - **Teinture** : 2 à 4 ml (environ 30 à 60 gouttes) 2 fois par jour.
- **Contre-indications & Précautions** :
  - 1. Abuta has been documented to lower blood pressure in two animal studies; therefore, abuta is probably contraindicated for people with low blood pressure. An alkaloid in abuta, tetrandrine, has been documented to have various actions on heart function in animals and humans. Those with a heart condition or taking heart medications should consult with their doctor before using this plant. 2. Abuta has demonstrated to be a uterine relaxant and traditionally employed as a childbirth aid. A pregnant woman should use it only under the supervision of a qualified healthcare practitioner. 3. In studies with mice, abuta extracts have demonstrated female anti-fertility activity by modulating the hormones specifically responsible for fertility. Those seeking to get pregnant should avoid the use of abuta.
- **Note Vitaliste** : Fiche botanique officielle Rain-Tree issue des recherches du Dr. Leslie Taylor sur la pharmacopée amazonienne.

  - were found to be the main constituents in both the leaves and fruits of caigua

A human study published in 1995 by Peruvian researchers reported that a dehydrated caigua fruit product reversed the changes in cholesterol experienced by post-menopausal women. The leaves of caigua are considered hypoglycemic and prepared in a decoc...

### 9. Amor seco (*Desmodium adscendens*)
- **Famille Botanique** : Plante Amazonienne
- **Noms Usuels / Synonymes** : Amor seco
- **Parties Utilisées** : Feuilles, écorce, racines ou plante entière
- **Catégorie Thérapeutique & Tropisme** : Reins & Émonctoires (Drainage Rénal & Lithiases)
- **Source Primaire Rain-Tree** : [Amor seco Monograph on Rain-Tree.com](https://www.rain-tree.com/amorseco.htm)
- **Principes Phytochimiques Actifs** :
  - Amor seco is known to be rich in flavonoids
  - alkaloids
  - glycosides and chemicals known as soyasaponins
  - A novel soyasaponin in amor seco is dehydrosoyasaponin 1
  - It is considered a highly active chemical with therapeutic actions for asthma
  - Amor seco also contains a chemical called astragalin
  - Amor seco’s traditional uses for infections
  - venereal diseases
- **Indications & Cibles Thérapeutiques** :
  - Today
  - A tea of the plant is given for nervousness
  - and it is used in baths to treat vaginal infections
  - Some tribes believe the plant has magic powers

  - an application of pounded leaves and lime juice for wounds

  - The latex is considered a poison and has caused deaths in Africa. Contact of the latex with the eyes can cause blindness. The caustic latex can also cause skin burns, ulcerations, and dermatitis. In one study, a water extract blocked the growth of rat prostate carcinoma; another study reported that a hot water extract of the entire plant i...

- **Famille Botanique** :

- **Principes Phytochimiques Actifs** :
  - Boldo has many biologically active chemicals
  - At least 17 alkaloids have been documented thus far
  - several of which are believed to be boldo's main active constituents
  - In various studies over the years boldine has shown to protect the liver
  - to stimulate the production of bile in the liver
  - as well as to stimulate digestion
  - In other laboratory tests
  - boldine has demonstrated diuretic
- **Indications & Cibles Thérapeutiques** :
  - Chemicals in boldo may thin the blood
  - chronic use
  - Do not exceed the recommended dosages
- **Mécanismes & Pharmacodynamie (Leslie Taylor)** :
1. **Actions Principales (Rain-Tree)** : 1. Boldo has demonstrated abortive properties and caused fetal birth defects in animal studies and therefore should not be used during pregnancy or while breast-feeding. 2. Chemicals in boldo may thin the blood. Those taking blood-thinning medications (such as Warfarin) or those with disorders that have a tendency towards thin blood (such as thrombocytopenia or hemophilia) should not take boldo unless under the supervision of a qualified healthcare practitioner. Boldo has diuretic effects and is contraindicated for long-term, chronic use. 4. Do not exceed the recommended dosages.
2. **Recherche Clinique & Activité Biologique** : Researchers verified indigenous uses of boldo leaves in the 1950s and 1960s and showed that leaf extracts had diuretic, digestion stimulation, and bile-producing properties in animal studies. Although these properties are attributed largely to the plant chemical boldine, one study with rats indicated that an alcohol extract of boldo leaves was more active than boldine alone. An ethanol extract of ...
3. **Usage Ethnobotanique Traditionnel** : Indigenous uses of boldo have been widely documented. Legend has it that the medicinal uses of the plant were discovered by chance: a Chilean shepherd noticed that his sheep were healthier, and had fewer liver problems, when they grazed on native boldo plants growing in his fields. Since this discovery the plant has been used by the indigenous peop...

  - **Infusion / Décoction** : 1 cuillère à café à soupe de plante séchée par tasse d'eau chaude, infuser 10-15 min.
  - **Teinture** : 2 à 4 ml (environ 30 à 60 gouttes) 2 fois par jour.
- **Contre-indications & Précautions** :
  - 1. Boldo has demonstrated abortive properties and caused fetal birth defects in animal studies and therefore should not be used during pregnancy or while breast-feeding. 2. Chemicals in boldo may thin the blood. Those taking blood-thinning medications (such as Warfarin) or those with disorders that have a tendency towards thin blood (such as thrombocytopenia or hemophilia) should not take boldo unless under the supervision of a qualified healthcare practitioner. Boldo has diuretic effects and is contraindicated for long-term, chronic use. 4. Do not exceed the recommended dosages.

- **Source Primaire Rain-Tree** : [Carqueja Monograph on Rain-Tree.com](https://www.rain-tree.com/carqueja.htm)
- **Principes Phytochimiques Actifs** :
  - Carqueja is a rich source of flavonoids
  - which are a type of polyphenol
  - Certain flavonoids
  - such as silymarin in milk thistle
  - Carqueja is rather like a South American version of milk thistle
  - It contains up to 20% flavonoids
  - including quercetin
  - luteolin
- **Indications & Cibles Thérapeutiques** :
  - Carqueja should not be used during pregnancy
  - As such
  - it is contraindicated in persons with hypoglycemia
- **Mécanismes & Pharmacodynamie (Leslie Taylor)** :
1. **Actions Principales (Rain-Tree)** : Carqueja should not be used during pregnancy, as it has demonstrated uterine stimulant and abortive effects in rats. The use of this plant is contraindicated in persons with low blood pressure due to its documented hypotensive effects. Persons with any heart condition or taking heart medications should check with their physician prior to using this plant. Carqueja has been documented to lower blood glucose levels in human and animal studies. As such, it is contraindicated in persons with hypoglycemia, and people with diabetes should check with their doctor prior to using this plant, and use with caution while monitoring their blood sugar levels accordingly.
2. **Recherche Clinique & Activité Biologique** : Carqueja's liver protective properties were confirmed in a clinical study when a crude flavonoid fraction of carqueja as well as a crude leaf/stem extract dose-dependently increased the survival rate to 100% in mice administered lethal dosages of phalloidin- a liver toxin (as compared to only a 24% survival rate in the control group). While these scientists indicated that the single flavonoid hisp...
3. **Usage Ethnobotanique Traditionnel** : Indigenous peoples of the rainforest have utilized this herb for centuries to cure common ailments. Its uses in herbal medicine were first recorded in Brazil in 1931 by Pio Correa, who wrote about an infusion of carqueja being used for sterility in women and impotency in men. Correa described carqueja as having the therapeutic properties of a tonic...
- **Posologie & Modes d'Administration** :
  - **Dosage Standard** : protects liver induces abortions Aerial parts detoxifies liver mildly laxative Infusion: 1/2 cup 2-3 times daily aids digestion increases urination Tincture: 2-4 ml 2-3 times daily reduces acid reduces fever Capsules: 2 g twice daily treats ulcers promotes sweating relieves pain expels worms kills viruses cleanses blood reduces free radicals lowers blood pressure lowers blood sugar protects organs tones gastric tract
  - **Infusion / Décoction** : 1 cuillère à café à soupe de plante séchée par tasse d'eau chaude, infuser 10-15 min.
  - **Teinture** : 2 à 4 ml (environ 30 à 60 gouttes) 2 fois par jour.

- **Noms Usuels / Synonymes** : Copaiba
- **Parties Utilisées** : Feuilles, écorce, racines ou plante entière
- **Catégorie Thérapeutique & Tropisme** : Reins & Émonctoires (Drainage Rénal & Lithiases)
- **Source Primaire Rain-Tree** : [Copaiba Monograph on Rain-Tree.com](https://www.rain-tree.com/copaiba.htm)
- **Principes Phytochimiques Actifs** :
  - The resin contains up to 15% volatile oil
  - the remaining materials are resins and acids
  - diterpenes
  - and terpenic acids
  - These chemicals include caryophyllene
  - calamenene
  - and copalic
  - coipaiferic
- **Indications & Cibles Thérapeutiques** :
  - Avoid contact with eyes and mucous membranes
  - as the resin can act as an irritant
  - Discontinue use if these effects occur
  - Do not take internally in large dosages (more than 5 ml)
  - Large dosages have been reported to cause nausea
  - vomiting
  - fever
  - and rashes
- **Mécanismes & Pharmacodynamie (Leslie Taylor)** :
1. **Actions Principales (Rain-Tree)** : 1. Avoid contact with eyes and mucous membranes, as the resin can act as an irritant. 2. Those sensitive to the resin may experience a measles-like rash accompanied by irritation, itching and/or tingling when using topically or taking internally. Discontinue use if these effects occur. 3. Do not take internally in large dosages (more than 5 ml). Large dosages have been reported to cause nausea, vomiting, fever, and rashes. Discontinue or reduce dosage if these effects occur. 4 One chemical in copaiba resin has been documented to cause hemolysis of red blood cells (human and mice) in vitro . Although this effect has not been studied in vivo, in the whole resin it is probably best to avoid long-term oral use of the resin unless you are under the direct care of a physician who can monitor this possible effect.
2. **Recherche Clinique & Activité Biologique** : Much of the clinical research performed to date has verified the traditional uses of copaiba. One of copaiba's main uses in herbal medicine is to help heal wounds. In 2002, researchers in Brazil first reported that it was highly effective as a topical wound healer in animal studies. Since then, six more in vivo animal studies have been published in 2009, 2010, 2013, and 2017 which confirm that usi...
3. **Usage Ethnobotanique Traditionnel** : On the Rio Solimoes in northwest Amazonia, copaiba resin is used topically by indigenous tribes as a wound healer, to stop bleeding, for skin sores and psoriasis, and to treat gonorrhea. Healers and curanderos in the Amazon today use copaiba resin for all types of pain, for skin disorders and insect bites, and to cool inflammation. In Brazilian her...
- **Posologie & Modes d'Administration** :
  - **Dosage Standard** : relieves pain increases urination Resin reduces inflammation expels worms Internal: 5-15 drops 2-3 kills germs reduces acid times daily kills bacteria supresses coughs External: apply diluted resin kills fungi expels phlegm on affected areas inhibits tumor growth dries secretions heals wounds protects gastric tract mildly laxative sooths and softens disinfects
  - **Infusion / Décoction** : 1 cuillère à café à soupe de plante séchée par tasse d'eau chaude, infuser 10-15 min.
  - **Teinture** : 2 à 4 ml (environ 30 à 60 gouttes) 2 fois par jour.
- **Contre-indications & Précautions** :
  - 1. Avoid contact with eyes and mucous membranes, as the resin can act as an irritant. 2. Those sensitive to the resin may experience a measles-like rash accompanied by irritation, itching and/or tingling when using topically or taking internally. Discontinue use if these effects occur. 3. Do not take internally in large dosages (more than 5 ml). Large dosages have been reported to cause nausea, vomiting, fever, and rashes. Discontinue or reduce dosage if these effects occur. 4 One chemical in copaiba resin has been documented to cause hemolysis of red blood cells (human and mice) in vitro . Although this effect has not been studied in vivo, in the whole resin it is probably best to avoid long-term oral use of the resin unless you are under the direct care of a physician who can monitor this possible effect.

### 60. Graviola (*Annona muricata*)
- **Famille Botanique** : Annonaceae
- **Noms Usuels / Synonymes** : Graviola, soursop, Brazilian paw paw, guan&aacute, bana, guan&aacute, bano, guanavana, guanaba, corossol &eacute, pineux, huanaba, toge-banreisi, durian benggala, nangka blanda, cachiman &eacute, pineux
- **Parties Utilisées** : 
- **Catégorie Thérapeutique & Tropisme** : Reins & Émonctoires (Drainage Rénal & Lithiases)
- **Source Primaire Rain-Tree** : [Graviola Monograph on Rain-Tree.com](https://www.rain-tree.com/graviola.htm)
- **Principes Phytochimiques Actifs** :
  - Many active compounds and chemicals have been found in graviola
  - as scientists have been studying its properties since the 1940s
  - Graviola produces these natural compounds in its leaf and stem
  - bark
  - fruit and fruit seeds
  - These chemicals in general have been documented with antitumorous
  - antiparasitic
  - insecticidal
- **Indications & Cibles Thérapeutiques** :
  - Graviola has demonstrated hypotensive
  - vasodilator
  - Chronic
  - Large single dosages may cause nausea or vomiting
  - Reduce the usage accordingly if this occurs
  - norepinephrine
  - and monomine oxidase activity
  - however
- **Mécanismes & Pharmacodynamie (Leslie Taylor)** :
1. **Actions Principales (Rain-Tree)** : 1. Graviola has demonstrated uterine stimulant activity in an animal study (rats) and should therefore not be used during pregnancy. 2. Graviola has demonstrated hypotensive, vasodilator, and cardiodepressant activities in animal studies and is contraindicated for people with low blood pressure. People taking antihypertensive drugs should check with their doctors before taking graviola and monitor their blood pressure accordingly (as medications may need adjusting). 3. Graviola has demonstrated significant in vitro antimicrobial properties. Chronic, long-term use of this plant may lead to die-off of friendly bacteria in the digestive tract due to its antimicrobial properties. Supplementing the diet with probiotics and digestive enzymes is advisable if this plant is used for longer than 30 days. 4. Animal studies indicate that graviola might lower blood sugar levels. Diabetics should use with caution while testing their glucose levels regularly to determine what effect it has as insulin medications may need adjusting. 5. Graviola has demonstrated emetic properties in one animal study with pigs. Large single dosages may cause nausea or vomiting. Reduce the usage accordingly if this occurs. 6. One study with rats given a stem-bark extract intragastrically (at 100 mg/kg) reported an increase in dopamine, norepinephrine, and monomine oxidase activity, as well as a inhibition of serotonin release in stress-induced rats. 7. Alcohol extracts of graviola leaf showed no toxicity or side effects in mice at 100 mg/kg; however, at a dosage of 300 mg/kg, a reduction in explorative behavior and mild abdominal constrictions was observed. If sedation or sleepiness occurs, reduce the amount used.
2. **Recherche Clinique & Activité Biologique** : In a 1976 plant screening program by the National Cancer Institute, graviola leaves and stem showed active toxicity against cancer cells and researchers have been following up on these findings since. Thus far, specific acetogenins in graviola and/or extracts of graviola have been reported to be selectively toxic in vitro to these types of tumor cells: lung carcinoma cell lines; human breast solid...
3. **Usage Ethnobotanique Traditionnel** : All parts of the graviola tree are used in natural medicine in the tropics, including the bark, leaves, roots, fruit, and fruit seeds. Different properties and uses are attributed to the different parts of the tree. Generally, the fruit and fruit juice are taken for worms and parasites, to cool fevers, to increase mother's milk after childbirth, an...
- **Posologie & Modes d'Administration** :
  - **Dosage Standard** : kills cancer cells relieves depression Leaves fights free radicals reduces anxiety Infusion: 1 cup 3 times daily kills bacteria kills viruses Tincture: 2-4 ml 3 times daily kills parasites reduces pain Capsules: 2 g 3 times daily reduces blood pressure expels worms relieves inflammation promotes weight loss protects cells & organs stops convulsions lowers blood sugar sedates relieves oxidative stress reduces spasms
  - **Infusion / Décoction** : 1 cuillère à café à soupe de plante séchée par tasse d'eau chaude, infuser 10-15 min.

- **Contre-indications & Précautions** :
  - 1. Graviola has demonstrated uterine stimulant activity in an animal study (rats) and should therefore not be used during pregnancy. 2. Graviola has demonstrated hypotensive, vasodilator, and cardiodepressant activities in animal studies and is contraindicated for people with low blood pressure. People taking antihypertensive drugs should check with their doctors before taking graviola and monitor their blood pressure accordingly (as medications may need adjusting). 3. Graviola has demonstrated significant in vitro antimicrobial properties. Chronic, long-term use of this plant may lead to die-off of friendly bacteria in the digestive tract due to its antimicrobial properties. Supplementing the diet with probiotics and digestive enzymes is advisable if this plant is used for longer than 30 days. 4. Animal studies indicate that graviola might lower blood sugar levels. Diabetics should use with caution while testing their glucose levels regularly to determine what effect it has as insulin medications may need adjusting. 5. Graviola has demonstrated emetic properties in one animal study with pigs. Large single dosages may cause nausea or vomiting. Reduce the usage accordingly if this occurs. 6. One study with rats given a stem-bark extract intragastrically (at 100 mg/kg) reported an increase in dopamine, norepinephrine, and monomine oxidase activity, as well as a inhibition of serotonin release in stress-induced rats. 7. Alcohol extracts of graviola leaf showed no toxicity or side effects in mice at 100 mg/kg; however, at a dosage of 300 mg/kg, a reduction in explorative behavior and mild abdominal constrictions was observed. If sedation or sleepiness occurs, reduce the amount used.

### 73. Jaborandi (*Pilocarpus jaborandi*)
- **Famille Botanique** : Plante Amazonienne
- **Noms Usuels / Synonymes** : Jaborandi, indian hemp, jaborandi-do-norte, catai-guacu, ibiratai, pimenta-de-cachorro, arruda do mato, arruda brava, jamguarandi, juarandi
- **Parties Utilisées** : Leaves, roots
- **Catégorie Thérapeutique & Tropisme** : Reins & Émonctoires (Drainage Rénal & Lithiases)
- **Source Primaire Rain-Tree** : [Jaborandi Monograph on Rain-Tree.com](https://www.rain-tree.com/jaborand.htm)
- **Principes Phytochimiques Actifs** :
  - to folklore use
  - and then into modern medicine based upon natural chemicals found in the plant
  - In 1875
  - In 1876
  - The mixture of pilocarpine and another natural product
  - physostigmine
  - remains to this day one of the mainstay drugs in ophthalmology
  - Interestingly
- **Indications & Cibles Thérapeutiques** :
  - An overdose may cause such symptoms as flushing
  - profuse sweating and salivation
  - urinary frequency
  - nausea
  - rapid pulse
  - contracted pupils
  - diarrhea or fatal pulmonary edema
  - The plant may induce bradycardia
- **Mécanismes & Pharmacodynamie (Leslie Taylor)** :
1. **Actions Principales (Rain-Tree)** : 1. Pilocarpine has shown to increase the rate of birth defects in animal studies. Jaborandi should not be taken during pregnancy or while breastfeeding. 2. Both jaborandi and pilocarpine may cause headaches and can irritate the stomach and cause vomiting and nausea. An overdose may cause such symptoms as flushing, profuse sweating and salivation, urinary frequency, nausea, rapid pulse, contracted pupils, diarrhea or fatal pulmonary edema. 3. The plant may induce bradycardia. Those with cardiac or circulatory conditions should not take jaborandi. 4. Jaborandi may induce dehydration due to excessive perspiration and urination. If using jaborandi, electrolyte and fluid status must be monitored and maintained.
2. **Recherche Clinique & Activité Biologique** : There are well over a thousand clinical studies published on pilocarpine. As with most plant-based drugs, however, the use of the whole natural plant fell out of use as a natural remedy (and failed to attract further research efforts) in favor of the single isolated active ingredient that was turned into a prescription drug. The PDR for Herbal Medicines indicates that the effects of jaborandi leav...
3. **Usage Ethnobotanique Traditionnel** : In 1570, Gabriel Soares de Souza, a European observer, first recorded that the Guarani Indians of Brazil were using jaborandi to treat mouth ulcers. In the 1630's two Dutch West Indian Company scientists documented other Brazilian Indians using it as a tonic or panacea for colds and flu, a remedy against gonorrhea and kidney stones and found that i...
- **Posologie & Modes d'Administration** :
  - **Dosage Standard** : reduces glacoma reduces inflammation Leaves promotes perspiration increases milk flow Not recommended increases saliva increases urination increases heart rate
  - **Infusion / Décoction** : 1 cuillère à café à soupe de plante séchée par tasse d'eau chaude, infuser 10-15 min.
  - **Teinture** : 2 à 4 ml (environ 30 à 60 gouttes) 2 fois par jour.
- **Contre-indications & Précautions** :
  - 1. Pilocarpine has shown to increase the rate of birth defects in animal studies. Jaborandi should not be taken during pregnancy or while breastfeeding. 2. Both jaborandi and pilocarpine may cause headaches and can irritate the stomach and cause vomiting and nausea. An overdose may cause such symptoms as flushing, profuse sweating and salivation, urinary frequency, nausea, rapid pulse, contracted pupils, diarrhea or fatal pulmonary edema. 3. The plant may induce bradycardia. Those with cardiac or circulatory conditions should not take jaborandi. 4. Jaborandi may induce dehydration due to excessive perspiration and urination. If using jaborandi, electrolyte and fluid status must be monitored and maintained.

- **Famille Botanique** :

  - though
  - The root of manacá contains coumarins

  - Manacá has a traditional use as an abortive
  - therefore
  - it is contraindicated for pregnant women
  - vertigo
  - general anesthesia
  - partial paralysis of the face
  - swollen tongue
  - and disturbed vision
- **Mécanismes & Pharmacodynamie (Leslie Taylor)** :
1. **Actions Principales (Rain-Tree)** : 1. Manacá has a traditional use as an abortive. No clinical studies have been performed to indicate its safety during pregnancy; therefore, it is contraindicated for pregnant women. 2. Manacá root is reported to have toxicity in large doses - causing excessive salivation, vertigo, general anesthesia, partial paralysis of the face, swollen tongue, and disturbed vision. Avoid dosages higher than the traditional remedy indicates. 3. Those allergic to aspirin (acetylsalicylic acid) should avoid using manacá. Manacá contains salicylate and several of its derivatives. Salicylate occurs naturally in plants; for some people, too much salicylate causes problems (known as "salicylate sensitivity" or "salicylate intolerance") without being allergic to aspirin. Do not use manacá if sensitive to salicylate. 4. Manacá root contains coumarins - plant chemicals known to thin the blood. Those taking blood-thinning medications such as coumadin should use manacá only under the direction and supervision of a qualified healthcare practitioner to monitor these effects. 5. The plant chemical scopoletin has been documented to inhibit monoamine oxidase. Those taking monoamine oxidase (MAO) inhibitors should consult their healthcare practitioner before taking manacá.
2. **Recherche Clinique & Activité Biologique** : Much more research is published on various chemicals found in manacá, rather than the plant itself. However, several animal studies do confirm some of its traditional uses - especially for pain and inflammation. In a 1991 clinical study with mice, manacá demonstrated pain-relieving and anti-inflammatory effects. An earlier (1977) study reported that manacá root extracts evidenced marked anti-infla...
3. **Usage Ethnobotanique Traditionnel** : Manacá has a long history of indigenous use for both medicine and magic. Its Brazilian common name, manacá , originated with the Tupi Indians in Brazil; they named it after the most beautiful girl in their tribe, Manacán, for its lovely flowers. It is a sacred and spiritual plant used by shamans and curanderos in the potion ayahuasca (a sacred hall...

  - **Teinture** : 2 à 4 ml (environ 30 à 60 gouttes) 2 fois par jour.
- **Contre-indications & Précautions** :
  - 1. Manacá has a traditional use as an abortive. No clinical studies have been performed to indicate its safety during pregnancy; therefore, it is contraindicated for pregnant women. 2. Manacá root is reported to have toxicity in large doses - causing excessive salivation, vertigo, general anesthesia, partial paralysis of the face, swollen tongue, and disturbed vision. Avoid dosages higher than the traditional remedy indicates. 3. Those allergic to aspirin (acetylsalicylic acid) should avoid using manacá. Manacá contains salicylate and several of its derivatives. Salicylate occurs naturally in plants; for some people, too much salicylate causes problems (known as "salicylate sensitivity" or "salicylate intolerance") without being allergic to aspirin. Do not use manacá if sensitive to salicylate. 4. Manacá root contains coumarins - plant chemicals known to thin the blood. - **Principes Phytochimiques Actifs** :
  - naturally occurring chemicals including flavonoids
  - alkaloids
  - and many different types of plant steroids
  - some of which have never before been seen in science
  - is toxic to numerous types of cancer and leukemia cells

  - and many of the documented anti-cancerous
- **Indications & Cibles Thérapeutiques** :
  - animal and human alike

These properties make it a promising candidate for further development in cancer therapeutics. Various extracts of mullaca have demonstrated potent activity against a wide range of human and animal cancer cells, including those from breast, brain, colorectal, gastric, ...
3. **Usage Ethnobotanique Traditionnel** : Mullaca has long held a place in natural medicine in the tropical countries where it grows. Its use by rainforest Indians in the Amazon is well documented, and its edible sweet-tart fruits are enjoyed by many rainforest inhabitants, animal and human alike. Indigenous tribes in the Amazon use a leaf infusion as a diuretic. Some Colombian tribes beli...
- **Posologie & Modes d'Administration** :
  - **Dosage Standard** : kills bacteria relieves pain Infusion: 1/2 to 1 cup 1-3 kills cancer cells reduces spasms times daily kills leukemia cells lowers blood sugar Capsules: 1-2 g twice daily fights free radicals prevents ulcers Tincture: 1-3 ml twice daily relieves inflammation increases urination kills viruses reduces fever modulates immune system protects cells & organs relieves oxidative stress kills parasites reduces fibrosis prevents diabetic complications lowers blood pressure

  - Several animal studies indicates this plant may lower blood pressure and one test tube study demonstrated a blood anticoagulant activity. People with blood disorders such as hemophilia, those taking heart medications or blood thinners, or those with other heart problems such as low blood pressure should not use this plant without supervision and advice of a qualified health care practitioner. Animal studies report that mullaca can lower blood glucose levels. Diabetics should use with caution and test their glucose levels regularly until they determine what effects mullaca might have on their glucose levels as diabetic medications may need adjusting.

### 103. Quebra Pedra (*Phyllanthus niruri*)
- **Famille Botanique** :

- **Parties Utilisées** : Feuilles, écorce, racines ou plante entière

  - Since the mid-1960s

  - stem
  - and root of the plant
  - including lignans
  - glycosides
  - flavonoids
  - alkaloids

  - While not studied specifically in humans or animals

  - however

  - It is contraindicated for people with hypoglycemia
  - however
- **Mécanismes & Pharmacodynamie (Leslie Taylor)** :
1. **Actions Principales (Rain-Tree)** : 1, Chanca piedra has demonstrated hypotensive effects in animals and humans. People with a heart condition and/or taking prescription heart medications should consult their doctor before taking this plant. It may be contraindicated for some individuals with heart conditions and/or heart medications may need monitoring and adjusting. 2. Chanca piedra has been considered in herbal medicine to be abortive (at high dosages) as well as a menstrual promoter. While not studied specifically in humans or animals, animal studies do indicate it has uterine relaxant effects. It should therefore be considered contraindicated during pregnancy. 3. Chanca piedra has been documented with female antifertility effects in one mouse study (the effect was reversed 45 days after cessation of dosing). While this effect has not been documented in humans, the use of the plant is probably contraindicated in women seeking pregnancy or taking fertility drugs. This effect has not been substantiated sufficiently to be used as a contraceptive, however, and should not be relied on for such. 4. Chanca piedra has demonstrated hypoglycemic effects in animals and humans. It is contraindicated for people with hypoglycemia. Diabetics should consult their doctor before taking this plant as insulin medications may need monitoring and adjusting. 5. Chanca piedra has been documented in human and animal studies with diuretic effects. Chronic and acute use of this plant may be contraindicated in various other medical conditions where diuretics are not advised. Chronic long-term use of any diuretic can cause electrolyte and mineral imbalances; however, human studies with chanca piedra (for up to three months of chronic use) has not reported any side effects. Consult your doctor if you choose to use this plant chronically for longer than three months concerning possible side effects of long term diuretic use.
2. **Recherche Clinique & Activité Biologique** : It is little wonder that chanca piedra is used for so many purposes in herbal medicine systems: in clinical research over the years, the plant has demonstrated liver protective, antilithic (expels stones), pain-relieving, hypotensive, antispasmodic, antiviral, antibacterial, diuretic, cancer preventative and anti-cancerous, cellular protective antioxidant, immunomodulator, anti-inflammatory and an...
3. **Usage Ethnobotanique Traditionnel** : The Spanish name of the plant, chanca piedra , means "stone breaker" or "shatter stone." It was named for its effective use to generations of Amazonian indigenous peoples in eliminating gallstones and kidney stones. In Brazil, the plant is known as quebra-pedra or arranca-pedras (which also translates to "break-stone"). In addition to kidney stones...

In the mid-1970s, rhizome extracts of samambaia were first reported to decrease the over-growth of...

In the Amazon rainforest a maceration of the rhizome is used for...
- **Posologie & Modes d'Administration** :
  - **Dosage Standard** : protects brain cells cleanses blood Leaves, Rhizome protects skin cells increaes urination Infusion: 1/2 to 1 cup 1-3 reduces inflammation lowers blood pressure times daily relieves psoriasis promotes perspiration Capsules: 1-2 g twice daily immunomodulator Tincture: 2-3 ml twice daily suppresses coughs reduces phlegm fights free radicals natural sunscreen

  - Reports indicate that samambaia may enhance the effects of the heart drug digitalis (a medication commonly used to increase the force of heart contractions in those diagnosed with certain heart conditions). - **Principes Phytochimiques Actifs** :
  - The main active group of chemicals in simarouba are called quassinoids
  - which belong to the triterpene chemical family
  - Quassinoids are found in many plants and are well known to scientists
  - Several of the quassinoids found in simarouba
  - such as ailanthinone
  - glaucarubinone
  - and holacanthone
  - anti-amebic
- **Indications & Cibles Thérapeutiques** :
  - When France suffered a dysentery epidemic from 1718 to 1725
  - simarouba bark was one of the few effective treatments
  - malaria
  - and dysentery
  - as a hemostatic agent to stop bleeding
  - and as a tonic
- **Mécanismes & Pharmacodynamie (Leslie Taylor)** :
1. **Actions Principales (Rain-Tree)** : Documenté par Leslie Taylor pour ses principes phytochimiques actifs.
2. **Recherche Clinique & Activité Biologique** : After a 200-year documented history of use for dysentery around the world, its use for amebic dysentery was finally validated by conventional doctors in 1918. A military hospital in England demonstrated that the bark tea was an effective treatment for amebic dysentery in humans. The Merck Institute reported that simarouba was 91.8% effective against intestinal amebas in humans in a 1944 study and,...
3. **Usage Ethnobotanique Traditionnel** : The leaves and bark of Simarouba have long been used as a natural medicine in the tropics. Simarouba was first imported into France from Guyana in 1713 as a remedy for dysentery. When France suffered a dysentery epidemic from 1718 to 1725, simarouba bark was one of the few effective treatments. French explorers "discovered" this effective remedy wh...
- **Posologie & Modes d'Administration** :
  - **Dosage Standard** : kills parasites relieves pain Bark kills amebas reduces fever Decoction: 1 cup 2-3 kills bacteria promotes menstruation times daily kills viruses hydrates skin Tincture: 5-10 ml twice daily relieves dystentery promotes perspiration nbsp; kills leukemia cells treats food poisoning treats malaria expels worms kills cancer cells reduces cancer recurrence
  - **Infusion / Décoction** : 1 cuillère à café à soupe de plante séchée par tasse d'eau chaude, infuser 10-15 min.
  - **Teinture** : 2 à 4 ml (environ 30 à 60 gouttes) 2 fois par jour.
- **Contre-indications & Précautions** :
  - While not studied in humans, simarouba may lower blood pressure (based on animal studies). Reported side effects at high dosages (approx. three times the traditional remedy) include increased perspiration and urination, nausea, and/or vomiting.

- **Catégorie Thérapeutique & Tropisme** : Reins & Émonctoires (Drainage Rénal & Lithiases)
- **Source Primaire Rain-Tree** : [Stevia Monograph on Rain-Tree.com](https://www.rain-tree.com/stevia.htm)
- **Principes Phytochimiques Actifs** :
  - Western interest in stevia began around the turn of the nineteenth century
  - It was first studied in 1899 by Paraguayan botanist Moises S
  - Bertoni
  - who wrote some of the earliest articles on stevia (in the early 1900s)
  - Over 100 phytochemicals have been discovered in stevia since
  - It is rich in terpenes and flavonoids
  - The constituents responsible for stevia's sweetness were documented in 1931
  - when eight novel plant chemicals called glycosides were discovered and named
- **Indications & Cibles Thérapeutiques** :
  - Stevia leaf has been documented to have a hypotensive
- **Mécanismes & Pharmacodynamie (Leslie Taylor)** :
1. **Actions Principales (Rain-Tree)** : 1. Stevia leaf (at dosages higher than used for sweetening purposes) has been documented to have a hypoglycemic effect. Those with diabetes should use high amounts of stevia with caution and monitor their blood sugar levels as medications may need adjusting. 2. Stevia leaf has been documented to have a hypotensive, or blood pressure lowering effect (at dosages higher than used for sweetening purposes) . Persons with low blood pressure and those taking antihypertensive drugs should avoid using large amounts of stevia and monitor their blood pressure levels accordingly for these possible effects.
2. **Recherche Clinique & Activité Biologique** : The great interest in stevia as a non-caloric, natural sweetener has fueled many studies on it - including toxicological ones. The main sweet chemical, stevioside, has been found to be nontoxic in acute toxicity studies with rats, rabbits, guinea pigs, and birds. It also has been shown not to cause cellular changes (mutagenic) or to have any effect on fertility. The natural stevia leaf also has be...
3. **Usage Ethnobotanique Traditionnel** : For hundreds of years, indigenous peoples in Brazil and Paraguay have used the leaves of stevia as a sweetener. The Guarani Indians of Paraguay call it kaa jheé and have used it to sweeten their yerba mate tea for centuries. They have also used stevia to sweeten other teas and foods and have used it medicinally as a cardiotonic, for obesity, hypert...
- **Posologie & Modes d'Administration** :
  - **Dosage Standard** : naturally sweetens kills bacteria Leaves lowers blood sugar kills fungi Ground leaves: 1/4 tsp = increases urination kills viruses 1 tsp of sugar lowers blood pressure reduces inflammation Infusion: 1 cup 2-3 dilates blood vessels times daily
  - **Infusion / Décoction** : 1 cuillère à café à soupe de plante séchée par tasse d'eau chaude, infuser 10-15 min.
  - **Teinture** : 2 à 4 ml (environ 30 à 60 gouttes) 2 fois par jour.
- **Contre-indications & Précautions** :
  - 1. Stevia leaf (at dosages higher than used for sweetening purposes) has been documented to have a hypoglycemic effect. Those with diabetes should use high amounts of stevia with caution and monitor their blood sugar levels as medications may need adjusting. 2. Stevia leaf has been documented to have a hypotensive, or blood pressure lowering effect (at dosages higher than used for sweetening purposes) . Persons with low blood pressure and those taking antihypertensive drugs should avoid using large amounts of stevia and monitor their blood pressure levels accordingly for these possible effects.
- **Note Vitaliste** : Fiche botanique officielle Rain-Tree issue des recherches du Dr. Leslie Taylor sur la pharmacopée amazonienne.

### 115. Suma (*Pfaffia paniculata*)
- **Famille Botanique** : Amaranthaceae
- **Noms Usuels / Synonymes** : Suma, Brazilian ginseng, pfaffia, para toda, corango-acu
- **Parties Utilisées** : 
- **Catégorie Thérapeutique & Tropisme** : Foie & Digestion (Hépato-Biliaire & Détox)
- **Source Primaire Rain-Tree** : [Suma Monograph on Rain-Tree.com](https://www.rain-tree.com/suma.htm)
- **Principes Phytochimiques Actifs** :
  - Nutritionally
  - suma root contains 19 different amino acids
  - a large number of electrolytes
  - trace minerals
  - iron
  - magnesium
  - zinc
  - vitamins A
- **Indications & Cibles Thérapeutiques** :
  - as well as to treat menopause
  - PMS
  - and other hormonal disorders
  - As such
  - avoid inhalation of the root powder/dust
  - Reduce dosages if these side effects are noted
- **Mécanismes & Pharmacodynamie (Leslie Taylor)** :
1. **Actions Principales (Rain-Tree)** : 1. Suma has been documented to contain a significant amount of plant sterols including a significant amount of beta-ecdysterone and small amounts of stigmasterol and beta-sitosterol. These sterols might have estrogenic properties or activities and/ or cause an increase in estrogen production (not clinically proven) as this plant has been used traditionally to regulate menstrual processes, as well as to treat menopause, PMS, and other hormonal disorders. As such, it is advisable for women with estrogen-positive cancers to avoid the use of this plant. 2. The root powder has been reported to cause asthmatic allergic reactions if inhaled. When handling raw suma root powder or preparing decoctions with root powder, avoid inhalation of the root powder/dust. 3. Ingestion of large amounts of plant saponins in general (naturally occurring chemicals in suma) has shown to sometimes cause mild gastric disturbances including nausea and stomach cramping. Reduce dosages if these side effects are noted.
2. **Recherche Clinique & Activité Biologique** : In addition to the pfaffic acids having anticancerous activity, research in Japan (in 2000) reported that natural suma root had anti-cancerous activity as well. In this in vivo study, an oral administration of powdered suma root (at a dosage of 750 mg/kg) was reported to inhibit the proliferation of lymphoma and leukemia in mice and, otherwise, delay mortality. Notice, however, that this antiproli...
3. **Usage Ethnobotanique Traditionnel** : In South America suma is known as para toda (which means "for all things") and as Brazilian ginseng , since it is widely used as an adaptogen with many applications (much as "regular" ginseng). The indigenous peoples of the Amazon region who named it para toda have used suma root for generations for a wide variety of health purposes, including as a...
- **Posologie & Modes d'Administration** :
  - **Dosage Standard** : supports hormones inhibits blood sickling Root adaptogen lowers cholesterol Decoction: 1 cup twice daily relieves pain calms nerves Capsules: 1-2 g twice daily reduces inflammation inhibits tumor growth inhibits cancer protects cells & organs kills leukemia cells oxygenates cells enhances immunity prevents muscle wasting treats erectile dysfunction prevents diseases encourages muscle growth relieves depression reduces stress
  - **Infusion / Décoction** : 1 cuillère à café à soupe de plante séchée par tasse d'eau chaude, infuser 10-15 min.
  - **Teinture** : 2 à 4 ml (environ 30 à 60 gouttes) 2 fois par jour.
- **Contre-indications & Précautions** :
  - 1. Suma has been documented to contain a significant amount of plant sterols including a significant amount of beta-ecdysterone and small amounts of stigmasterol and beta-sitosterol. These sterols might have estrogenic properties or activities and/ or cause an increase in estrogen production (not clinically proven) as this plant has been used traditionally to regulate menstrual processes, as well as to treat menopause, PMS, and other hormonal disorders. As such, it is advisable for women with estrogen-positive cancers to avoid the use of this plant. 2. The root powder has been reported to cause asthmatic allergic reactions if inhaled. When handling raw suma root powder or preparing decoctions with root powder, avoid inhalation of the root powder/dust. 3. Ingestion of large amounts of plant saponins in general (naturally occurring chemicals in suma) has shown to sometimes cause mild gastric disturbances including nausea and stomach cramping. Reduce dosages if these side effects are noted.

### 117. Ubos (*Spondias mombin*)
- **Famille Botanique** : 
- **Noms Usuels / Synonymes** : Ubos
- **Parties Utilisées** : Feuilles, écorce, racines ou plante entière
- **Catégorie Thérapeutique & Tropisme** : Reins & Émonctoires (Drainage Rénal & Lithiases)
- **Source Primaire Rain-Tree** : [Ubos Monograph on Rain-Tree.com](https://www.rain-tree.com/ubos.htm)
- **Principes Phytochimiques Actifs** :
  - Ubos leaves and bark contains tannins
  - saponins
  - flavonoids
  - sterols
  - quinones
  - and antioxidant chemicals
  - This natural plant chemical has been reported with antibacterial
  - antiviral
- **Indications & Cibles Thérapeutiques** :
  - Ubos bark is traditionally used as a contraceptive
  - Ubos leaves contains salicylic acid derivatives
  - If allergic or sensitive to aspirin or salicylic acid
  - avoid use of the leaves
  - Drug Interactions: None reported
  - Worldwide Ethnomedical Uses Africa for bleeding
  - childbirth
  - chronic diarrhea
- **Mécanismes & Pharmacodynamie (Leslie Taylor)** :
1. **Actions Principales (Rain-Tree)** : 1. Ubos leaves are traditionally used as a childbirth aid to induce labor and as an abortive. Animals studies report uterine stimulant and abortive actions. Do not use if you are pregnant unless under the direct supervision of a medical practitioner. 2. Ubos bark is traditionally used as a contraceptive. While no animal or human studies support this traditional use, women seeking to become pregnant should probably avoid use of this plant. 3. Ubos leaves contains salicylic acid derivatives. If allergic or sensitive to aspirin or salicylic acid, avoid use of the leaves. Drug Interactions: None reported. Animal studies document ubos leaves with sedative and anti-anxiety effects and as such, the leaves might enhance the effect of other sedative and anti-anxiety medications. Worldwide Ethnomedical Uses Africa for bleeding, childbirth, chronic diarrhea, constipation, coughs, diarrhea, fever, gonorrhea, postpartum hemorrhage, stomach problems, tapeworm, and yaws Belize for diarrhea, dysentery, gonorrhea, inflammation, insect bites, sores, sore throat, rashes, weakness, and wounds Bolivia for injuries and wounds Brazil as an anthelmintic, antibacterial, anti-inflammatory. antispasmodic, antiviral, astringent, diuretic, emetic, molluscicidal, refrigerant, stimulant, stomachic, tonic, and vermifuge; The use of ubos leaves by midwives has been studied and reported over several year...
3. **Usage Ethnobotanique Traditionnel** : Ubos is widely relied on for various herbal remedies for numerous conditions and virtually every part of the tree is used — from its thick corky bark, to its leaves, fruits, roots, to even its flowers. Usually different properties and actions are attributed to different parts of the tree. In South American herbal medicine systems as well as in othe...
- **Posologie & Modes d'Administration** :
  - **Dosage Standard** : kills bacteria calms & sedates Leaves kills viruses kills parasites Infusion: 1 cup 2-3 times daily reduces anxiety relieves pain Capsules: 1-2 g twice daily aids digestion suppresses cough kills candida stimulates uterus expels worms stops convulsions
  - **Infusion / Décoction** : 1 cuillère à café à soupe de plante séchée par tasse d'eau chaude, infuser 10-15 min.
  - **Teinture** : 2 à 4 ml (environ 30 à 60 gouttes) 2 fois par jour.
- **Contre-indications & Précautions** :
  - 1. Ubos leaves are traditionally used as a childbirth aid to induce labor and as an abortive. Animals studies report uterine stimulant and abortive actions. Do not use if you are pregnant unless under the direct supervision of a medical practitioner. 2. Ubos bark is traditionally used as a contraceptive. While no animal or human studies support this traditional use, women seeking to become pregnant should probably avoid use of this plant. 3. Ubos leaves contains salicylic acid derivatives. If allergic or sensitive to aspirin or salicylic acid, avoid use of the leaves.

  - Antioxidant chemicals (tannins
  - catechins and procyanidins) as well as plant sterols (beta-sitosterol

  - anti-inflammatory

  - However
  - and it should not be relied on for such
- **Mécanismes & Pharmacodynamie (Leslie Taylor)** :
1. **Actions Principales (Rain-Tree)** : 1. Cat's claw has been clinically documented with immunostimulant effects and is contraindicated before or following any organ or bone marrow transplant or skin graft. 2. Cat's claw has been documented with antifertility properties and is contraindicated in persons seeking to get pregnant. However, this effect has not been proven to be sufficient for the product to be used as a contraceptive, and it should not be relied on for such. 3, Cat's claw has chemicals that can reduce platelet aggregation and thin the blood. Check with your doctor first if you are taking coumadin or other blood-thinning drugs and discontinue use one week to ten days prior to any major surgical procedure. 4. Cat's claw vine bark requires sufficient stomach acid to help break down the tannins and alkaloids during digestion and to aid in absorption. Avoid taking bark capsules or tablets at the same time as antacids. Avoid taking high tannin (dark-colored) liquid extracts and tinctures directly by mouth and dilute first in water or acidic juice (such as orange juice). 5. Large dosages of cat's claw (3-4 gram dosages at a time) have been reported to cause some abdominal pain or gastrointestinal problems, including diarrhea (due to the tannin content of the vine bark) in some people. The diarrhea or loose stools tend to be mild and go away with continued use. Discontinue use or reduce dosage if diarrhea persists longer than three or four days.
2. **Recherche Clinique & Activité Biologique** : With so many documented traditional uses of this important rainforest plant, it is not surprising that it came to the attention of Western researchers and scientists. Studies began in the early 1970s when Klaus Keplinger, a journalist and self-taught ethnologist from Innsbruck, Austria, organized the first definitive work on cat's claw. Keplinger's work in the 1970s and 1980s led to several extrac...
3. **Usage Ethnobotanique Traditionnel** : Both South American Uncaria species are used by the indigenous peoples of the Amazon rainforest in very similar ways and have long histories of use. Cat's claw (U. tomentosa) has been used medicinally by the Aguaruna, Ashaninka, Cashibo, Conibo, and Shipibo tribes of Peru for at least 2,000 years. The Ashaninka Indian tribe in central Peru has the ...

  - The seeds of velvet bean are high in protein
  - carbohydrates

  - They are also rich in novel alkaloids
  - saponins
  - and sterols

  - It should not be used during pregnancy
  - Velvet bean has shown to lower blood sugar

  - inhibitors

  - increasing testosterone levels
  - Velvet bean inhibits prolactin
  - The seed contains high quantities of L-dopa
- **Mécanismes & Pharmacodynamie (Leslie Taylor)** :
1. **Actions Principales (Rain-Tree)** : 1. The seed may cause birth defects and has uterine stimulant activity. It should not be used during pregnancy. 2. Velvet bean has shown to lower blood sugar. Those with hypoglycemia or diabetes should only use Velvet bean under the supervision of a qualified healthcare practitioner. 3. Velvet bean is contraindicated in combination with M.A.O. inhibitors. 4. Velvet bean has androgenic activity, increasing testosterone levels. Persons with excessive androgen syndromes should avoid using Velvet bean. 5. Velvet bean inhibits prolactin. If you have a medical condition resulting in inadequate levels of prolactin in the body, do not use Velvet bean unless under the direction or your healthcare practitioner. 6. The seed contains high quantities of L-dopa. Levodopa is the pharmaceutical medication used for Parkinson's disease. Those with Parkinson's should only use velvet bean under the supervision of a qualified healthcare practitioner.
2. **Recherche Clinique & Activité Biologique** : Velvet bean has demonstrated little toxicity; however, it has been documented in animal studies to cause birth defects and should not be used during pregnancy. Traditionally, velvet bean has been used as a nerve tonic for nervous system disorders. Due to the high concentration of L-dopa in the seeds, it has been studied for its possible use in Parkinson's disease. Parkinson's disease is a common a...
3. **Usage Ethnobotanique Traditionnel** : In Central America, velvet beans have been roasted and ground to make a coffee substitute for decades; its goes by the common name of "nescafé" in these regions, as well as in Brazil, for this reason. It is still grown as a food crop by the Ketchi indigenous people in Guatemala; the bean is cooked as a vegetable. In Brazil the seed has been used in...

  - then held over an open fire (to fire-cure the leaves)
- **Mécanismes & Pharmacodynamie (Leslie Taylor)** :
1. **Actions Principales (Rain-Tree)** : Documenté par Leslie Taylor pour ses principes phytochimiques actifs.
2. **Recherche Clinique & Activité Biologique** : One of the largest areas of research on yerba mate is the confirmation of the plant’s strong antioxidant actions. More than 50 human, animal and test tube studies reveal how yerba mate can reduce or prevent unstable molecules (called free radicals or reactive oxygen species) from harming various cells and organs in the body (called oxidative stress). The damage caused by oxidative stress can be a ...
3. **Usage Ethnobotanique Traditionnel** : Yerba mate was has been used as a beverage since the time of the ancient Indians of Brazil and Paraguay. In the early 16th century, Juan de Solís, a Spanish explorer of South America's famed La Plata River, reported that the Guarani Indians of Paraguay brewed a leaf tea that "produced exhilaration and relief from fatigue." The Spaniards tried the b...
- **Posologie & Modes d'Administration** :
  - **Dosage Standard** : increases energy relieves pain Leaves promotes weight loss increases bile Infusion: 1 cup 2-3 suppresses appetite mildly laxative times daily burns fat protects liver Capsules: 1-2 g twice daily reduces inflammation reduces blood pressure modulates immune system enhances immunity prevents chronic diseases protects brain protects heart relieves depression fights free radicals reduces blood sugar avoids diabetic complications protects bones relieves oxidative stress enhances memory
  - **Infusion / Décoction** : 1 cuillère à café à soupe de plante séchée par tasse d'eau chaude, infuser 10-15 min.
  - **Teinture** : 2 à 4 ml (environ 30 à 60 gouttes) 2 fois par jour.
- **Contre-indications & Précautions** :
  - Yerba mate contains caffeine and should not be used by those who are sensitive or allergic to caffeine. Excessive consumption of caffeine is contraindicated for persons with high blood pressure, diabetes, ulcers, and other diseases. Yerba mate tea should not be consumed excessively and chronically (as it has been linked to possibly increasing the risk of certain such cancers as oral and esophageal cancer). Taking yerba mate in capsules instead of hot teas avoids this possible risk. Yerba mate has been reported to have MAO-inhibitor activity. Those persons taking MAO-inhibitor drugs should use yerba mate with caution to monitor these possible effects. Yerba mate has been reported to lower blood pressure in human studies. Those taking medications to lower blood pressure should use with caution until they determine if medications need adjusting. Yerba mate has been reported to lower blood glucose levels in human studies. Diabetics should consult with their health care provider to determine if diabetes medications need adjusting while taking yerba mate.
- **Note Vitaliste** : Fiche botanique officielle Rain-Tree issue des recherches du Dr. Leslie Taylor sur la pharmacopée amazonienne.

  - These compounds help gives the tuber its sweet flavor however

2. **Recherche Clinique & Activité Biologique** : Yacon root contains a high concentration of substances called fructooligosaccharides (FOS) and inulin, which are types of prebiotic soluble fibers. FOS are not digested in the upper gastrointestinal tract, so they don’t spike blood glucose levels. They reach the colon intact and feed specific beneficial gut bacteria which improves insulin sensitivity. Several species of bacteria are responsible fo...
3. **Usage Ethnobotanique Traditionnel** : In early evolutionary periods, Andean farmers had already recognized the value of yacon as a cultivated food crop. Yacon has been discovered in burial grounds from centuries before the Incas. The first written record of yacon was in 1615 when Felipe Guaman Poma de Ayala included it in a list of 55 native crops cultivated by the Andeans. In South Am...

Diabetics should check with a practitioner before using the leaves and monitor their blood sugar levels accordingly as medications may need adjustment. ### 127. Nettle (*Urtica dioica*)
- **Famille Botanique** : Urticaceae
- **Noms Usuels / Synonymes** : Nettle
- **Parties Utilisées** : Feuilles, écorce, racines ou plante entière
- **Catégorie Thérapeutique & Tropisme** : Reins & Émonctoires (Drainage Rénal & Lithiases)
- **Source Primaire Rain-Tree** : [Nettle Monograph on Rain-Tree.com](https://www.rain-tree.com/nettles.htm)
- **Principes Phytochimiques Actifs** :
  - histamine
  - serotonin
  - and choline
  - In addition to these chemicals
  - nettle leaf is rich in minerals
  - chlorophyll
  - amino acids
  - lecithin
- **Indications & Cibles Thérapeutiques** :
  - Nettle has been documented to have diuretic effects
- **Mécanismes & Pharmacodynamie (Leslie Taylor)** :
1. **Actions Principales (Rain-Tree)** : 1. Nettle has been documented in animal studies to lower blood pressure and heart rate. Those with heart conditions should seek the advice and supervision of a health practitioner to determine if nettle is suitable for their condition and to monitor its effects. 2. Nettle has been documented to have diuretic effects. Chronic use of this plant may be contraindicated in various medical conditions where diuretics are not advised. Chronic long-term use of any diuretic can cause electrolyte and mineral imbalances. Consult your doctor if you choose to use this plant chronically for longer than 30 days concerning possible side effects of long term diuretic use.
2. **Recherche Clinique & Activité Biologique** : Nettle's long-standing use as an anti-inflammatory aid for rheumatism and arthritis has been confirmed with clinical research. In several clinical studies (including a randomized double-blind placebo trial) nettle leaf extracts were documented with anti-inflammatory actions as well as to be beneficial (and better than placebo) at relieving arthritis pain and inflammation in humans. Research sugges...
3. **Usage Ethnobotanique Traditionnel** : In folk medicine nettle plants have been used as a diuretic, to build the blood, for arthritis and rheumatism. Externally it has been used to improve the appearance of the hair, and is said to be a remedy against oily hair and dandruff. The plant has been widely used by herbalists around the world for centuries. In the first century, Greek physicia...

  - 1. Nettle has been documented in animal studies to lower blood pressure and heart rate. Those with heart conditions should seek the advice and supervision of a health practitioner to determine if nettle is suitable for their condition and to monitor its effects. 2. Nettle has been documented to have diuretic effects. Chronic use of this plant may be contraindicated in various medical conditions where diuretics are not advised.
